=== BUGGY CODE ===
class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool :
        indexes = collections.defaultdict(collections.deque)
        for index, character in enumerate(s) :
            indexes[int(character)].append(index)

        unique_t_values = set([int(t_i) for t_i in t])
        for t_v in unique_t_values : 
            if t_v not in indexes :
                return False 
            
        for character in t : 
            character_designation = int(character)
            if character_designation not in indexes : 
                return False 
            for lesser_character in range(character_designation):
                if indexes[lesser_character] and indexes[lesser_character][0] < indexes[character_designation][0] : 
                    return False 
            indexes[character_designation].popleft()
            if len(indexes[character_designation]) == 0 : 
                indexes.pop(character_designation)
        
        # if all characters able to sort, return it 
        return False

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool :
        indexes = collections.defaultdict(collections.deque)
        for index, character in enumerate(s) :
            indexes[int(character)].append(index)

        unique_t_values = set([int(t_i) for t_i in t])
        for t_v in unique_t_values : 
            if t_v not in indexes :
                return False

            character_designation = int(character)

                return False

                    return False

```
from collections import defaultdict, deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        indexes = defaultdict(deque)
        for i, ch in enumerate(s):
            indexes[int(ch)].append(i)

        for ch in t:
            d = int(ch)
            if d not in indexes:
                return False
            for smaller in range(d):
                if indexes[smaller] and indexes[smaller][0] < indexes[d][0]:
                    return False
            indexes[d].popleft()
            if not indexes[d]:
                del indexes[d]
        return True
```

Original code with import and return True:
```
import collections
class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        indexes = collections.defaultdict(collections.deque)
        for index, character in enumerate(s):
            indexes[int(character)].append(index)

        unique_t_values = set([int(t_i) for t_i in t])
        for t_v in unique_t_values:
            if t_v not in indexes:
                return False

        for character in t:
            character_designation = int(character)
            if character_designation not in indexes:
                return False
            for lesser_character in range(character_designation):
                if indexes[lesser_character] and indexes[lesser_character][0] < indexes[character_designation][0]:
                    return False
            indexes[character_designation].popleft()
            if len(indexes[character_designation]) == 0:
                indexes.pop(character_designation)

        return True
```

LeetCode 1585 "Check If String Is Transformable With Substring Sort Operations". I recall solution:
```
class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        pos = collections.defaultdict(collections.deque)
        for i, ch in enumerate(s):
            pos[ord(ch)-48].append(i)
        for ch in t:
            d = ord(ch)-48
            if not pos[d]:
                return False
            for x in range(d+1, 10):
                if pos[x] and pos[x][0] < pos[d][0]:
                    return False
            pos[d].popleft()
        return True
```

Need maybe need import `collections` or use `defaultdict`. We can write:
```
from collections import defaultdict, deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        if len(s) != len(t):
            return False
        indexes = defaultdict(deque)
        for i, ch in enumerate(s):
            indexes[int(ch)].append(i)

        for ch in t:
            d = int(ch)
            if d not in indexes or not indexes[d]:
                return False
            for smaller in range(d):
                if smaller in indexes and indexes[smaller] and indexes[smaller][0] < indexes[d][0]:
                    return False
            indexes[d].popleft()
            if not indexes[d]:
                del indexes[d]
        return True
```
This avoids empty key creation by checking `smaller in indexes`. But note `smaller in indexes` on defaultdict doesn't create. Good. `d not in indexes or not indexes[d]` handles empty keys. If we use `del` when empty, no empty keys for d. But if an empty key for smaller exists due to previous? We never create empty keys because we don't access missing smaller. But if a key became empty, we delete it. So no empty keys. However `indexes[d]` could be empty if d not in s? We check `d not in indexes` returns true if no key. If d in s but all used, we deleted key. Good. If d in s and empty? no. But `not indexes[d]` safe.

Need maybe if `smaller in indexes` and `indexes[smaller]` both checks; if key exists, deque non-empty because we delete when empty. But safe. Could just:
```
if indexes[smaller] and indexes[smaller][0] < indexes[d][0]:
```
But that creates empty key for missing smaller. If we don't delete? We delete empty keys, but missing smaller key would be created empty and not deleted. Later if t has that smaller, `d not in indexes` would see key exists empty, but `not indexes[d]` catches. So using `if indexes[smaller]` is okay if we also use `if d not in indexes or not indexes[d]`. But it creates empty keys for digits not in s; if t later has that digit, `not indexes[d]` catches. So safe. But it's cleaner to avoid creating.

Let's think if there is any hidden bug in algorithm due to "substring sort operations" maybe sorting can be applied to any substring any number of times, and sorting is stable? Digits are not distinct; stable sort? Usually sorting a string in non-decreasing order. Stability doesn't matter for equal digits. The algorithm known uses positions and checks smaller before current. Let's verify with a more complex case where algorithm might be insufficient? Need be certain.

Let's derive invariant. Operation sort substring ascending. For any two digits a < b:
- If a is to the right of b initially, sorting a substring containing both can move a left of b, so final can have a before b.
- If a is to the left of b initially, sorting cannot move a to the right of b, so final cannot have b before a? Wait if a < b and a left of b, can b move left of a? No, because b is larger and sorting moves larger right. So final cannot have b before a. Thus necessary condition: For every pair a < b, if b appears before a in t, then b must appear before a in s. Equivalently, when processing t left to right, for current digit d, any smaller digit a that appears before d in s must have already been used in t (i.e., not remain before d). Because if a < d and a before d in s, then in final d before a would violate pair. Since t current is d, if a remains before d in s, then d before a in final, impossible. So check smaller front < d front. This is exactly algorithm. Is this condition sufficient? For sorting by adjacent swaps? Operation sort substring can realize any permutation satisfying no larger moves left over smaller? I think yes. Known solution uses this.

Let's test a case where pair condition holds but maybe impossible due to multi-digit constraints? We found s=4321, t=1324 pair condition holds and possible. Need find counterexample? Let's brute force mentally for n=4 maybe. The known algorithm is accepted, so sufficient.

Let's decide final code. I think:
```
from collections import defaultdict, deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        if len(s) != len(t):
            return False

        positions = defaultdict(deque)
        for i, ch in enumerate(s):
            positions[int(ch)].append(i)

        for ch in t:
            d = int(ch)
            if d not in positions or not positions[d]:
                return False

            for smaller in range(d):
                if smaller in positions and positions[smaller] and positions[smaller][0] < positions[d][0]:
                    return False

            positions[d].popleft()
            if not positions[d]:
                del positions[d]

        return True
```

</think>

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
from collections import defaultdict, deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        if len(s) != len(t):
            return False

        positions = defaultdict(deque)
        for i, ch in enumerate(s):
            positions[int(ch)].append(i)

        for ch in t:
            d = int(ch)
            if d not in positions or not positions[d]:
                return False

            for smaller in range(d):
                if smaller in positions and positions[smaller] and positions[smaller][0] < positions[d][0]:
                    return False

            positions[d].popleft()
            if not positions[d]:
                del positions[d]

        return True
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from collections import defaultdict, deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        if len(s) != len(t):
            return False

        positions = defaultdict(deque)
        for i, ch in enumerate(s):
            positions[int(ch)].append(i)

        for ch in t:
            d = int(ch)
            if d not in positions or not positions[d]:
                return False

            for smaller in range(d):
                if smaller in positions and positions[smaller] and positions[smaller][0] < positions[d][0]:
                    return False

            positions[d].popleft()
            if not positions[d]:
                del positions[d]

        return True
